---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n411\t\n412\t\n413\tclass TestParallelClampAtFifteen:\n414\t    def test_parallel_sixteen_clamps_to_fifteen(\n415\t        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n416\t    ) -> None:\n417\t        \"\"\"``parallel=16`` saturates to 15; no worker observes a 16th peer.\"\"\"\n418\t\n419\t        observed_concurrency: list[int] = []\n420\t        concurrency_lock = threading.Lock()\n421\t        isolations_seen: dict[str, HomeIsolation] = {}\n422\t        isolations_lock = threading.Lock()\n423\t\n424\t        # Use a hold-event to keep every worker alive until the test\n425\t        # releases them, so the concurrency snapshot has a chance to\n426\t        # observe the upper-bound peer count. Without the barrier the\n427\t        # workers complete fast enough that the snapshot under-counts.\n428\t        hold_event = threading.Event()\n429\t        worker = _make_isolation_worker(\n430\t            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n431\t            config=eval_config,\n432\t            observed_concurrency=observed_concurrency,\n433\t            concurrency_lock=concurrency_lock,\n434\t            isolations_seen=isolations_seen,\n435\t            isolations_lock=isolations_lock,\n436\t            hold_event=hold_event,\n437\t        )\n438\t\n439\t        # Submit 20 specs at parallel=16; if the clamp leaks we'll\n440\t        # observe more than 15 workers running concurrently. Once the\n441\t        # peak is observed we release the barrier so the pool drains.\n442\t        specs = _build_specs(20)\n443\t        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n444\t\n445\t        # Release the barrier on a delay so workers actually pile up\n446\t        # before any of them finish. The delay is generous (1s) but\n447\t        # bounded so a hung worker cannot block the test indefinitely.\n448\t        releaser = threading.Timer(1.0, hold_event.set)\n449\t        releaser.start()\n450\t        try:\n451\t            outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=16)\n452\t        finally:\n453\t            hold_event.set()  # unconditionally release in case of failure\n454\t            releaser.cancel()\n455\t\n456\t        assert len(outcomes) == 20\n457\t        # The clamp is the *only* thing that can keep observed_concurrency\n458\t        # bounded at 15: ``ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=16)`` would\n459\t        # have admitted a 16th worker before any of them finished.\n460\t        assert max(observed_concurrency) <= RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL, (\n461\t            f\"observed concurrency peaked at {max(observed_concurrency)} \"\n462\t            f\"workers; clamp at {RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL} leaked\"\n463\t        )\n464\t        # Sanity check that the test actually achieved parallelism — if\n465\t        # the snapshot peaks at 1 the assertion above is vacuous.\n466\t        assert max(observed_concurrency) > 1, (\n467\t            \"concurrency snapshot is degenerate;            assert event[\"env_home\"] == event[\"home_path\"]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# AC: parallel=16 clamps to MAX_PARALLEL=15\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestParallelClampAtFifteen:\n    def test_parallel_sixteen_clamps_to_fifteen(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"``parallel=16`` saturates to 15; no worker observes a 16th peer.\"\"\"\n\n        observed_concurrency: list[int] = []\n        concurrency_lock = threading.Lock()\n        isolations_seen: dict[str, HomeIsolation] = {}\n        isolations_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n        # Use a hold-event to keep every worker alive until the test\n        # releases them, so the concurrency snapshot has a chance to\n        # observe the upper-bound peer count. Without the barrier the\n        # workers complete fast enough that the snapshot under-counts.\n        hold_event = threading.Event()\n        worker = _make_isolation_worker(\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            config=eval_config,\n            observed_concurrency=observed_concurrency,\n            concurrency_lock=concurrency_lock,\n            isolations_seen=isolations_seen,\n            isolations_lock=isolations_lock,\n            hold_event=hold_event,\n        )\n\n        # Submit 20 specs at parallel=16; if the clamp leaks we'll\n        # observe more than 15 workers running concurrently. Once the\n        # peak is observed we release the barrier so the pool drains.\n        specs = _build_specs(20)\n        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n\n        # Release the barrier on a delay so workers actually pile up\n        # before any of them finish. The delay is generous (1s) but\n        # bounded so a hung worker cannot block the test indefinitely.\n        releaser = threading.Timer(1.0, hold_event.set)\n        releaser.start()\n        try:\n            outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=16)\n        finally:\n            hold_event.set()  # unconditionally release in case of failure\n            releaser.cancel()\n\n        assert len(outcomes) == 20\n        # The clamp is the *only* thing that can keep observed_concurrency\n        # bounded at 15: ``ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=16)`` would\n        # have admitted a 16th worker before any of them finished.\n        assert max(observed_concurrency) <= RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL, (\n            f\"observed concurrency peaked at {max(observed_concurrency)} \"\n            f\"workers; clamp at {RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL} leaked\"\n        )\n        # Sanity check that the test actually achieved parallelism — if\n        # the snapshot peaks at 1 the assertion above is vacuous.\n        assert max(observed_concurrency) > 1, (\n            \"concurrency snapshot is degenerate; clamp assertion is vacuous\"\n        )\n\n    def test_parallel_at_max_admits_fifteen_concurrent_workers(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"``parallel=15`` actually admits all 15 workers simultaneously.\"\"\"\n\n        observed_concurrency: list[int] = []\n        concurrency_lock = threading.Lock()\n        isolations_seen: dict[str, HomeIsolation] = {}\n        isolations_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n        hold_event = threading.Event()\n        worker = _make_isolation_worker(\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            config=eval_config,\n            observed_concurrency=observed_concurrency,\n            concurrency_lock=concurrency_lock,\n            isolations_seen=isolations_seen,\n            isolations_lock=isolations_lock,\n            hold_event=hold_event,\n        )\n\n        specs = _build_specs(15)\n        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n        releaser = threading.Timer(1.0, hold_event.set)\n        releaser.start()\n        try:\n            outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=15)\n        finally:\n            hold_event.set()\n            releaser.cancel()\n\n        assert len(outcomes) == 15\n        # Confirms the clamp does not under-admit either: when the\n        # request equals MAX_PARALLEL, all 15 workers should run\n        # concurrently. When the token\n        is set, the orchestrator stops submitting new work and\n        synthesises an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome for every spec that was\n        never scheduled.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    DEFAULT_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 8\n    MIN_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 1\n    MAX_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 15\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        run_one: EvalWorker,\n        cancellation_token: Optional[CancellationToken] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        if not callable(run_one):\n            raise TypeError(\"run_one must be callable\")\n        self._run_one = run_one\n        self._cancellation_token = cancellation_token\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Public surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def run(\n        self,\n        specs: Sequence[EvalSpec],\n        *,\n        parallel: int = DEFAULT_PARALLEL,\n    ) -> list[EvalOutcome]:\n        \"\"\"Execute every spec in parallel and return one outcome per spec.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        specs:\n            Expanded :class:`EvalSpec` sequence (post FR-EXP1 expansion).\n            The returned list preserves this order so the Reporter\n            renders rows deterministically.\n        parallel:\n            Requested concurrency level. Clamped to\n            ``[MIN_PARALLEL, MAX_PARALLEL]``; values below the minimum\n            raise :class:`ValueError`, values above the maximum saturate\n            to ``MAX_PARALLEL``. Defaults to :attr:`DEFAULT_PARALLEL`.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        list[EvalOutcome]\n            One :class:`EvalOutcome` per input spec, in the input order.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # The minimum-bound rejection is the only hard failure: a\n        # negative or zero concurrency value is almost certainly a\n        # caller bug (CLI argument parsing should have caught it\n        # earlier) and silently coercing it would mask the bug. An\n        # upper-bound value, by contrast, is the documented behaviour\n        # of design-spec §11 — \"parallel=20 clamps to 15\" — and the\n        # orchestrator must honour it without raising.\n        if not isinstance(parallel, int) or isinstance(parallel, bool):\n            raise TypeError(\n                f\"parallel must be int; got {type(parallel).__name__}\"\n            )\n        if parallel < self.MIN_PARALLEL:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"parallel must be >= {self.MIN_PARALLEL}; got {parallel}\"\n            )\n        max_workers = min(parallel, self.MAX_PARALLEL)\n\n        if not specs:\n            return []\n\n        # Pre-allocate the result slots so we can fill them by index as\n        # futures complete. ``as_completed`` yields in completion order,\n        # not submission order, so we cannot append blindly.\n        outcomes: list[Optional[EvalOutcome]] = [None] * len(specs)\n\n        # Indices for specs that were never submitted because the token\n        # was already cancelled when the scheduler reached them. They\n        # receive a synthesised ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome below.\n        cancelled_indices: list[int] = []\n\n        with ThreadPoolExecutor(\n            max_workers=max_workers, thread_name_prefix=\"cliEval\"\n        ) as pool:\n            futures: dict[Future[EvalOutcome], int] = {}\n\n            for index, spec in enumerate(specs):\n                # Cooperative cancellation: once the token flips, stop\n                # submitting new work. Already-running workers observe\n                # the token themselves (via :class:`EvalRunner`) and\n                # convert to INTERRUPTED outcomes; we synthesise an\n                # INTERRUPTED row for every spec that did not even\n                # reach the pool so the per-spec contract still holds.\n                if self._is_cancelled():\n                    cancelled_indices.append(index)\n                    continue\n                future = pool.submit(self._invoke_worker, spec)\n                futures[future] = index\n\n            # Drain the pool. ``as_completed`` is the AC6 idiom — it\n            # yields each future the instant its worker returns,\n            # letting downstream readers (telemetry, TUI) update in\n            # near real-time without waiting for the slowest eval.\n            for future in as_completed(futures):\n                index = futures[future]\n                spec = specs[index]\n                try:\n                    outcomes[index] = future.result()\n                except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — see docstring\n                    # The worker should fold its own harness errors\n                    # into an ERRORED outcome via :func:`run_eval`. We\n                    # only get here when the worker itself raised\n                    # (construction failure, unwired-token\n                    # KeyboardInterrupt propagation, etc.). Translate\n                    # to ERRORED so :meth:`run` never re-raises.\n                    outcomes[index] = self._errored_outcome(spec, exc)\n\n        # Backfill INTERRUPTED outcomes for never-submitted specs after\n        # the pool has shut down so we cannot race with an in-flight\n        # cancellation.\n        for index in cancelled_indices:\n            outcomes[index] = self._interrupted_outcome(specs[index])\n\n        # Every slot must be populated by now. The cast filters the\n        # ``Optional[...]`` typing without runtime cost; the assertion\n        # guards against a future regression.\n        assert all(o is not None for o in outcomes), (\n            \"RunOrchestrator: one or more outcome slots were left empty\"\n        )\n        return [o for o in outcomes if o is not None]\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _invoke_worker(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Indirection so subclasses / tests can hook the call site.\"\"\"\n        return self._run_one(spec)\n\n    def _is_cancelled(self) -> bool:\n        token = self._cancellation_token\n        return token is not None and token.is_cancelled()\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _interrupted_outcome(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Build the synthesised ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome for a skipped spec.\n\n        Mirrors :meth:`EvalRunner._make_interrupted_outcome` so the\n        reporter cannot distinguish an in-flight cancellation from an\n        orchestrator-level one — both shapes are byte-identical.\n        \"\"\"\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"INTERRUPTED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=None,\n        )\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _errored_outcome(spec: EvalSpec, exc: BaseException) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Fold a worker exception into a DM-001 ``ERRORED`` outcome.\n\n        ``error_class`` is the fully-qualified exception classname so\n        the Reporter can group identical harness failures across runs\n        without parsing free-form messages. Matches the format\n        :func:`run_eval` uses for in-runner harness failures.\n        \"\"\"\n        cls = type(exc)\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"ERRORED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n        )\n","numLines":292,"startLine":1,"totalLines":292}}}

_evalspec_to_dict(spec: EvalSpec) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project an ``EvalSpec`` into a manifest-shaped dict.\n\n    Field ordering matches ``suite.schema.json`` so YAML/JSON output is\n    deterministic hard_failures=tuple(hard_failures),\n        soft_skips=tuple(soft_skips),\n        soft_xfails=tuple(soft_xfails),\n    )\n\n\ndef build_doctor_report(\n    skip_flags: Optional[Iterable[str]] = _evalspec_to_dict(spec: EvalSpec) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project an ``EvalSpec`` into a manifest-shaped dict.\n\n    Field ordering matches ``suite.schema.json`` so YAML/JSON output is\n    deterministic    \"no editorial transformation\" guarantee from T01.22 Notes holds: an\n    eval that did not declare ``category:`` in YAML does not gain one\n    here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    payload: dict[str, Any] = dict(spec.isolation)\n    if spec.inputs:\n        payload[\"inputs\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.inputs]\n    if spec.expects:\n        payload[\"expects\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.expects]\n match wins):\n\n    1. ``token`` is itself a file path that exists → return it.\n    2. ``suites_dir / f\"{token}.yaml\"`` exists → return it (filename stem\n       convention used by the built-in suites).\n    3. CLI\n            callers map this to :data:`SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    candidate = Path(suite)\n    if candidate.is_file():\n        return candidate\n\n    by_filename = suites_dir / f\"{suite}.yaml\"\n    if by_filename.is_file():\n        return by_filename\n\n    loader = SuiteLoader()\n    for manifest_path in discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir):\n        try:\n            parsed = loader.load(manifest_path)\n        except SuiteLoaderError:\n            # Neighbour manifest is broken; don't let it mask the lookup.\n            continue\n        if parsed.name == suite:\n            return manifest_path\n\n    raise SuiteNotFound(suite, suites_dir)\n\n\ndef describe_suite(\n    suite: str,\n    *,\n    suites_dir: Path,\n    eval_id: Optional[str] = None,\n    loader: Optional[SuiteLoader] = None,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Resolve, load, and project a suite (optionally a single eval).\n\n    Validation runs via :class:`SuiteLoader` BEFORE any rendering, so\n    schema / id-regex / capability rejections raise before the caller\n    can produce stdout (FR-CLI3 AC: validate before print).\n\n    Args:\n        suite: Suite name, filename stem, or direct manifest path. passed=passed,\n        failure_mode=\"hard\",\n        description=description,\n        detail=detail,\n    )\n\n\ndef _check_claude_home(home: Optional[Path] = None) -> CapabilityStatus:\n    \"\"\"HARD check: ``~/.claude/`` (or override) exists and is a directory.\"\"\"\n    target = home if home is not None else Path.home() / \".claude\"\n    exists = target.is_dir()\n    return CapabilityStatus(\n        name=\"filesystem.claude_home\",\n        passed=exists,\n        failure_mode=\"hard\",\n        description=\"~/.claude/ directory exists\",\n        detail=str(target) if exists else f\"{target} not found\",\n    )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# NFR-PERF2 free-RAM precheck (T03.17 / D-0059)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#\n# Design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) pins peak resident-set-size at 2.25 GB for\n# ``--parallel 15``. When an operator requests the maximum concurrency on a\n# host that cannot afford that ceiling, the doctor MUST emit a warning\n# string containing the literal ``\"2.25 GB\"`` token so the operator can\n# tell at a glance which budget was insufficient. Hosts with less than\n# 4 GB free RAM are out-of-scope per ``CLAUDE.md`` infrastructure notes —\n# the warning is the documented stop-gap, not an exit-2 hard failure\n# (which would block legitimate ``--parallel 8`` runs on tight hosts).\n\nRAM_CEILING_GB: float = 2.25\n\"\"\"Design-spec §11 NFR-PERF2 peak-RSS ceiling at ``--parallel 15``.\n\nThe literal value ``2.25`` (not the bytes) is the one operators see in\nthe warning text, so changing it requires a roadmap edit. The bytes\nconstant below is derived from it so the two views cannot drift.\n\"\"\"\n\nRAM_CEILING_BYTES: int = int(RAM_CEILING_GB * (1024**3))\n\"\"\"Byte-equivalent of :data:`RAM_CEILING_GB` for free-RAM comparisons.\n\n``int(2.25 * 1024**3) == 2_415_919_104``. Computed once at import time so\nthe helper does not re-derive it on every call.\n\"\"\"\n\nRAM_CEILING_TEXT: str = f\"{RAM_CEILING_GB} GB\"\n\"\"\"Canonical rendering of the ceiling used in operator-facing strings.\n\nThe T03.17 AC asserts that the warning contains this literal token. Pin\nit here so any downstream string formatting picks up changes uniformly.\n\"\"\"\n\nPARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD: int = 15\n\"\"\"``--parallel`` value at-or-above which the RAM precheck fires.\n\nMatches :attr:`RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL`. Below this threshold the\nbenchmark does not document a ceiling, so the doctor stays silent rather\nthan emit a misleading number.\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef _default_free_ram_probe() -> Optional[int]:\n    \"\"\"Return free RAM in bytes via Linux ``/proc/meminfo``.\n\n    Prefers ``MemAvailable`` (kernel ≥3.14) because it accounts for\n    reclaimable cache the way an oncoming allocation would; falls back to\n    ``MemFree`` on ancient kernels. Returns ``None`` on any failure (eg\n    non-Linux host, locked-down container) so the doctor records a\n    ``probe unavailable`` SOFT-SKIP rather than crashing — a doctor that\n    crashes on tight hosts is worse than one that warns.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    meminfo = Path(\"/proc/meminfo\")\n    if not meminfo.is_file():\n        return None\n    try:\n        text = meminfo.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n    available: Optional[int] = None\n    free: Optional[int] = None\n    for line in text.splitlines():\n        key, _, rest = line.partition(\":\")\n        key = key.strip()\n        if key not in (\"MemAvailable\", \"MemFree\"):\n            continue\n        tokens = rest.strip().split()\n        if not tokens:\n            continue\n        try:\n            kb = int(tokens[0])\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        unit = tokens[1].lower() if len(tokens) > 1 else \"kb\"\n        # /proc/meminfo always reports in kB on Linux; the unit check is\n        # defensive in case a future kernel changes the format.\n        multiplier = 1024 if unit == \"kb\" else 1\n        value = kb * multiplier\n        if key == \"MemAvailable\":\n            available = value\n        elif key == \"MemFree\":\n            free = value\n    if available is not None:\n        return available\n    return free\n\n\ndef _check_free_ram_for_parallel(\n    *,\n    requested_parallel: Optional[int],\n    probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[int]]] = None,\n    ceiling_bytes: int = RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n    threshold_parallel: int = PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD,\n) -> Optional[CapabilityStatus]:\n    \"\"\"SOFT-SKIP check: free RAM is sufficient for the requested concurrency.\n\n    Returns ``None`` (gate inactive) when ``requested_parallel`` is below\n    :data:`PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD` — the design-spec only pins the\n    RSS ceiling for the upper-bound run, so warning operators about\n    insufficient RAM at ``--parallel 8`` would be noise.\n\n    When the gate is active the helper returns a :class:`CapabilityStatus`\n    whose ``failure_mode`` is ``\"skip\"`` regardless of outcome:\n\n    * Passed when the probe returns a value at or above\n      :data:`RAM_CEILING_BYTES`.\n    * Failed (SOFT-SKIP) when free RAM is below the ceiling, with the\n      detail string containing the literal :data:`RAM_CEILING_TEXT` token\n      so a grep against the operator-facing artifact pins the failure\n      mode without parsing structure.\n    * Failed (SOFT-SKIP) when the probe returned ``None`` — the host\n      cannot self-report free RAM so we cannot certify the budget either.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if requested_parallel is None or requested_parallel < threshold_parallel:\n        return None\n\n    probe = probe or _default_free_ram_probe\n    description = (\n        f\"free RAM >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n    )\n    try:\n        free_ram = probe()\n    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — probe is caller-supplied\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free-RAM probe raised {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}; \"\n                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget unverified\"\n            ),\n        )\n    if free_ram is None:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free-RAM probe unavailable on this host; cannot verify \"\n                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget\"\n            ),\n        )\n    free_gb = free_ram / (1024**3)\n    if free_ram < ceiling_bytes:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB < {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} ceiling \"\n                f\"for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n            ),\n        )\n    return CapabilityStatus(\n        name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n        passed=True,\n        failure_mode=\"skip\",\n        description=description,\n        detail=f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT}\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _check_ptytest_vendored(pty_dir: Optional[Path] = None) -> CapabilityStatus:\n    \"\"\"SOFT-SKIP check: ptytest vendored under ``cli/eval/pty/``.\n\n    The actual ptytest vendoring lands at M2; the row stays SOFT-SKIP so\n    doctor still exits 0 on a clean dev machine until the upstream task\n    materialises ``cli/eval/pty/__init__.py``.\n    \"\"\"\n    target = pty_dir if f\"{init_file} not found (vendored at M2)\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _extend_report(\n    base: CapabilityReport,\n    extras: tuple[CapabilityStatus, ...],\n) -> CapabilityReport:\n    \"\"\"Return a new status.failure_mode == \"hard\":\n            hard_failures.append(status.name)\n        elif status.failure_mode == \"skip\":\n            soft_skips.append(status.name)\n        else:  # \"xfail\"\n            soft_xfails.append(status.name)\n    return replace(\n        base,\n        report=rows,\n        hard_failures=tuple(hard_failures),\n        soft_skips=tuple(soft_skips),\n        soft_xfails=tuple(soft_xfails),\n    )\n\n\ndef build_doctor_report(\n    skip_flags: Optional[Iterable[str]] = None,\n    *,\n    gates: Optional[CapabilityGates] = None,\n    claude_version_probe: _evalspec_to_dict(spec: EvalSpec) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project an ``EvalSpec`` into a manifest-shaped dict.\n\n    Field ordering matches ``suite.schema.json`` so YAML/JSON output is\n    deterministic _, rest = line.partition(\":\")\n        key = key.strip()\n        if key not in (\"MemAvailable\", \"MemFree\"):\n            continue\n        tokens = rest.strip().split()\n        if not tokens:\n            continue\n        try:\n            kb = int(tokens[0])\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        unit = tokens[1].lower() if len(tokens) > 1 else \"kb\"\n        # /proc/meminfo always reports in kB on Linux; the unit check is\n        # defensive in case a future kernel changes the format.\n        multiplier = 1024 if unit == \"kb\" else 1\n        value = kb * multiplier\n        if key == \"MemAvailable\":\n            available = value\n        elif key == \"MemFree\":\n            free = value\n    if available is not None:\n        return available\n    return free\n\n\ndef _check_free_ram_for_parallel(\n    *,\n    requested_parallel: Optional[int],\n    probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[int]]] = None,\n    ceiling_bytes: int =  Returns ``None`` (gate inactive) when ``requested_parallel`` is below\n    :data:`PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD` — the design-spec only pins the\n    RSS ceiling for the upper-bound run, so warning operators about\n    insufficient RAM at ``--parallel 8`` would be noise.\n\n    When the gate is active the helper returns a :class:`CapabilityStatus`\n    whose ``failure_mode`` is ``\"skip\"`` regardless of outcome:\n\n    * Passed when the probe returns a value at or above\n      :data:`RAM_CEILING_BYTES`.\n    * Failed (SOFT-SKIP) when free RAM is below the ceiling, with the\n      detail string containing the literal :data:`RAM_CEILING_TEXT` token\n      so a grep against the operator-facing artifact pins the failure\n      mode without parsing structure.\n    * Failed (SOFT-SKIP) when the probe returned ``None`` — the host\n      cannot self-report free RAM so we cannot certify the budget either.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if requested_parallel is None or requested_parallel < threshold_parallel:\n        return None\n\n    probe = probe or _default_free_ram_probe\n    description = (\n        f\"free RAM >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n    )\n    try:\n        free_ram = probe()\n    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — probe is caller-supplied\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free-RAM probe raised {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}; \"\n                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget unverified\"\n            ),\n        )\n    if free_ram is None:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free-RAM probe unavailable on this host; cannot verify \"\n                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget\"\n            ),\n        )\n    free_gb = free_ram / (1024**3)\n    if free_ram < ceiling_bytes:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB < {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} ceiling \"\n                f\"for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n            ),\n        )\n    return CapabilityStatus(\n        name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n        passed=True,\n        failure_mode=\"skip\",\n        description=description,\n        detail=f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT}\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _check_ptytest_vendored(pty_dir: Optional[Path] = None) -> CapabilityStatus:\n    \"\"\"SOFT-SKIP check: ptytest vendored under ``cli/eval/pty/``.\n\n    The actual ptytest vendoring lands at M2; the row stays SOFT-SKIP so\n    doctor still exits 0 on a clean dev machine until the upstream task\n    materialises ``cli/eval/pty/__init__.py``.\n    \"\"\"\n    target = pty_dir if pty_dir is not None else Path(__file__).resolve().parent / \"pty\"\n    init_file = target / \"__init__.py\"\n    passed = init_file.is_file()\n    return CapabilityStatus(\n        name=\"vendored.ptytest\",\n        passed=passed,\n        failure_mode=\"skip\",\n        description=\"ptytest vendored under cli/eval/pty/\",\n        detail=str(target) if passed else f\"{init_file} not found (vendored at M2)\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _extend_report(\n    base: CapabilityReport,\n    extras: tuple[CapabilityStatus, ...],\n) -> CapabilityReport:\n    \"\"\"Return a new ``CapabilityReport`` with ``extras`` appended and\n    failure-bucket tallies updated.\n\n    The base report's ``blocked_evals`` and ``skip_flags`` are preserved\n    verbatim — supplementary doctor rows only contribute to the three\n    failure buckets (``hard_failures``, ``soft_skips``, ``soft_xfails``).\n    \"\"\"\n    rows = base.report + extras\n    hard_failures = list(base.hard_failures)\n    soft_skips = list(base.soft_skips)\n    soft_xfails = list(base.soft_xfails)\n    for status in extras:\n        if status.passed:\n            continue\n        if status.failure_mode == \"hard\":\n            hard_failures.append(status.name)\n        elif status.failure_mode == \"skip\":\n            soft_skips.append(status.name)\n        else:  # \"xfail\"\n            soft_xfails.append(status.name)\n    return replace(\n        base,\n        report=rows,\n        hard_failures=tuple(hard_failures),\n        soft_skips=tuple(soft_skips),\n        soft_xfails=tuple(soft_xfails),\n    )\n\n\ndef build_doctor_report(\n    skip_flags: Optional[Iterable[str]] = None,\n    *,\n    gates: Optional[CapabilityGates] = None,\n    claude_version_probe: _evalspec_to_dict(spec: EvalSpec) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project an ``EvalSpec`` into a manifest-shaped dict.\n\n    Field ordering matches ``suite.schema.json`` so YAML/JSON output is\n    deterministic and round-trips visually against the source manifest.\n    Optional fields are omitted when they hold their dataclass default\n    (e.g. ``category=\"\"``, empty tuples, ``timeout_sec=None``) so the\n    \"no editorial transformation\" guarantee from T01.22 Notes holds: an\n    eval that did not declare ``category:`` in YAML does not gain one\n    here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    payload: dict[str, Any] = {\n        \"id\": spec.id,\n        \"title\": spec.title,\n    }\n    if spec.category:\n        payload[\"category\"] = spec.category\n    if spec.requires:\n        payload[\"requires\"] = list(spec.requires)\n    if spec.timeout_sec is not None:\n        payload[\"timeout_sec\"] = spec.timeout_sec\n    if spec.isolation is not None:\n        payload[\"isolation\"] = dict(spec.isolation)\n    if spec.inputs:\n        payload[\"inputs\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.inputs]\n    if spec.expects:\n        payload[\"expects\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.expects]\n    if spec.parameterize:\n        payload[\"parameterize\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.parameterize]\n    return payload\n\n\ndef _parsed_suite_to_dict(parsed: ParsedSuite) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project a :class:`ParsedSuite` into the manifest envelope shape.\n\n    ``evals`` is the post-parameterize-expansion list (the canonical\n    \"what would run if you invoked ``eval run``\" view        \"name\": parsed.name,\n        \"version\": parsed.version,\n        \"description\": parsed.description,\n        \"defaults\": dict(parsed.defaults),\n        \"required_binaries\": parsed.evals],\n    }\n\n\ndef resolve_suite_manifest(suite: str, suites_dir: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve ``--suite <token>`` to a manifest path on disk.\n\n    Resolution precedence (first match wins):\n\n    1. ``token`` is itself a file path that exists → return it.\n    2. ``suites_dir / f\"{token}.yaml\"`` exists → return it (filename stem\n       convention used by the built-in suites).\n    3. Otherwise scan ``suites_dir`` and return the first manifest whose\n       schema-validated ``name`` field equals ``token``. Broken manifests\n       in the same directory are skipped silently — we only fail on the\n       *requested* suite, not on the neighbours.\n\n    Raises:\n        SuiteNotFound: No manifest matched the token under any rule. CLI\n            callers map this to :data:`SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    candidate = Path(suite)\n    if candidate.is_file():\n        return candidate\n\n    by_filename = suites_dir / f\"{suite}.yaml\"\n    if by_filename.is_file():\n        return by_filename\n\n    loader = SuiteLoader()\n    for manifest_path in discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir):\n        try:\n            parsed = loader.load(manifest_path)\n        except SuiteLoaderError:\n            # Neighbour manifest is broken; don't let it mask the lookup.\n            continue\n        if parsed.name == suite:\n            return manifest_path\n\n    raise SuiteNotFound(suite, suites_dir)\n\n\ndef describe_suite(\n    suite: str,\n    *,\n    suites_dir: Path,\n    eval_id: Optional[str] = None,\n    loader: Optional[SuiteLoader] = None,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Resolve, load, and project a suite (optionally a single eval).\n\n    Validation runs via :class:`SuiteLoader` BEFORE any rendering, so\n    schema / id-regex / capability rejections raise before the caller\n    can produce stdout (FR-CLI3 AC: validate before print).\n\n    Args:\n        suite: Suite name, filename stem, or direct manifest path. See\n            :func:`resolve_suite_manifest` for resolution precedence.\n        suites_dir: Directory to search when ``suite`` is not a direct\n            path. Tests inject a tmp dir via ``--suites-dir``.\n        eval_id: Optional post-expansion eval id (``E1``, ``E2.1``) to\n            filter the output to a single eval. Missing → ``EvalNotFound``.\n        loader: Injectable :class:`SuiteLoader` for tests that need to\n            stub capability resolution.\n\n    Returns:\n        A dict suitable for ``yaml.safe_dump`` / ``json.dumps``. When\n        ``eval_id`` is ``None``, the dict is the full manifest envelope;\n        when ``eval_id`` is set, the dict is a single ``evalEntry`` shape\n        (the same projection :func:`_evalspec_to_dict` produces).\n\n    Raises:\n        SuiteNotFound: Resolution rule chain produced no manifest.\n        EvalNotFound: ``eval_id`` did not match any post-expansion id.\n        SchemaError / InvalidEvalId / UnresolvedCapability: Propagated\n            from :class:`SuiteLoader` so the CLI exit-code map still\n            applies (all → 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    manifest_path = resolve_suite_manifest(suite, suites_dir)\n    active_loader = loader if loader is not None else SuiteLoader()\n    parsed: ParsedSuite = active_loader.load(manifest_path)\n\n    if eval_id is None:\n        return _parsed_suite_to_dict(parsed)\n\n    for spec in parsed.evals:\n        if spec.id == eval_id:\n            return _evalspec_to_dict(spec)\n    raise EvalNotFound(\n \" (xfail)\"\n\n\ndef render_checklist(report: CapabilityReport) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the doctor green-checklist text (FR-CLI4 human output).\"\"\"\n    lines = [\"superclaude eval doctor:\"]\n _evalspec_to_dict(spec: EvalSpec) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project an ``EvalSpec`` into a manifest-shaped dict.\n\n    Field ordering matches ``suite.schema.json`` so YAML/JSON output is\n    deterministic {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01LG2mJhi3NFfw2PWXcs1pZY","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01QutPX3w6GiEZHm6iMvC2x5","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/tests/cli/eval/test_perf_resource_bounds.py","content":"\"\"\"NFR-PERF2 resource-bound benchmark: peak RSS at ``--parallel 15``.\n\nTask T03.17 / Deliverable D-0059 / Roadmap row R-059.\n\nThis module is the empirical leg of the NFR-PERF2 contract pinned by\ndesign-spec §11:\n\n    \"Peak resident-set size MUST stay <= 2.25 GB when ``--parallel 15``\n     is in effect on the dev host (linux, 4 GB+ free RAM).\"\n\nThe doctor-side precheck (T03.17, see :mod:`commands._check_free_ram_for_parallel`)\nwarns operators *before* a run starts; this file *measures* the actual\nresident footprint when the orchestrator is at saturation, so a future\nregression that doubles the per-worker RAM cost (eg. a leaky telemetry\nbuffer) shows up here instead of as a SIGKILL in CI.\n\nMethodology\n===========\n\nThe benchmark composes :class:`RunOrchestrator` (T03.15 / D-0057) over a\nstub worker — same shape as ``test_parallel_15.py``'s isolation worker —\nbecause the design-spec ceiling is per-orchestration-process, not per\nsubprocess. A real ``claude`` subprocess would add hundreds of megabytes\nof RSS that belong to the upstream binary, not the harness; pinning the\nharness ceiling without that contamination is the goal.\n\nPeak RSS is sampled via :func:`resource.getrusage` (POSIX stdlib) on\n:data:`resource.RUSAGE_SELF`. ``ru_maxrss`` is the high-water mark of\nresident memory since process start — exactly the \"peak RSS during the\nrun\" semantics the AC needs. On Linux the field is reported in\n**kilobytes**; on macOS in bytes. The helper :func:`_ru_maxrss_bytes`\nnormalises both views.\n\nThe benchmark records the *delta* between the high-water mark before the\norchestrator runs and the high-water mark after it returns. Doing this\nensures the ceiling we report is attributable to the orchestrator+worker\ncomposition rather than to whatever pytest infrastructure happens to be\nloaded by the time the test starts.\n\nHost gating\n===========\n\n``CLAUDE.md`` documents that hosts with less than 4 GB free RAM are\nout-of-scope for the upper-bound bench. When ``/proc/meminfo`` reports\nless than that we ``pytest.xfail`` with the documented host limitation\nrather than failing the suite — the AC explicitly carves out this case.\n\nEvidence\n========\n\nThe benchmark writes a ``perf-ram.json`` report describing the peak RSS\ndelta, the ceiling, the host's free-RAM snapshot, and the pass/xfail\nstatus. The destination is configurable via the\n``CLIEVAL_PERF_RAM_REPORT_PATH`` environment variable so the CI invocation\ncan drop the artifact at\n``.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T03.17/perf-ram.json`` without\nthe test having to know that path. When the env var is absent the report\nis written to a per-test temporary directory so the test still produces a\nverifiable artifact in plain pytest runs.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport os\nimport platform\nimport resource\nimport threading\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.commands import (\n    RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n    RAM_CEILING_TEXT,\n)\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.models import EvalOutcome, EvalSpec\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.orchestrator import RunOrchestrator\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# RSS sampling helpers\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _ru_maxrss_bytes() -> int:\n    \"\"\"Return ``ru_maxrss`` in bytes regardless of platform units.\n\n    POSIX is inconsistent here: Linux reports kilobytes, macOS reports\n    bytes. The platform sniff is cheap and the alternative — assuming\n    Linux units silently — would have the benchmark over-report by 1024x\n    on a developer's mac and trip the ceiling.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    raw = resource.getrusage(resource.RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss\n    if platform.system() == \"Darwin\":\n        return int(raw)\n    return int(raw) * 1024\n\n\ndef _free_ram_bytes() -> int | None:\n    \"\"\"Snapshot host free RAM from ``/proc/meminfo``; ``None`` off-Linux.\n\n    Mirrors :func:`commands._default_free_ram_probe` so the benchmark and\n    the doctor precheck consult the same source. We re-implement instead\n    of importing the private helper to keep this file's contract on\n    public surface only.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    meminfo = Path(\"/proc/meminfo\")\n    if not meminfo.is_file():\n        return None\n    try:\n        text = meminfo.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n    available: int | None = None\n    free: int | None = None\n    for line in text.splitlines():\n        key, _, rest = line.partition(\":\")\n        key = key.strip()\n        if key not in (\"MemAvailable\", \"MemFree\"):\n            continue\n        tokens = rest.strip().split()\n        if not tokens:\n            continue\n        try:\n            kb = int(tokens[0])\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        if key == \"MemAvailable\":\n            available = kb * 1024\n        else:\n            free = kb * 1024\n    return available if available is not None else free\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Stub worker (mirrors test_parallel_15's shape, simplified)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _make_stub_worker(\n    *,\n    hold_event: threading.Event,\n    payload_bytes: int = 1024,\n) -> Any:\n    \"\"\"Return a worker that allocates a small payload + waits on a barrier.\n\n    The payload is intentionally tiny: the benchmark measures the\n    orchestrator's per-worker footprint, not the worker's. Holding every\n    worker on an Event until the test releases them lets the snapshot\n    actually see all 15 workers resident at once instead of catching\n    them sequentially.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def worker(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        # A 1 KiB blob keeps the worker honest about touching memory\n        # without dominating the RSS measurement.\n        _ = bytes(payload_bytes)\n        # Park here so the orchestrator's pool is at saturation when\n        # the main thread samples ``ru_maxrss``. The 10 s ceiling\n        # prevents a hung test from blocking CI forever — the timer in\n        # the test releases the event well within that bound.\n        hold_event.wait(timeout=10.0)\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"PASS\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=None,\n        )\n\n    return worker\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Evidence sink\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _report_destination(tmp_path: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve where to write ``perf-ram.json`` for this run.\n\n    Honours ``CLIEVAL_PERF_RAM_REPORT_PATH`` so the M3 evidence harvest\n    can drop the artifact under\n    ``.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T03.17/`` without the test\n    itself having to know that path. Falls back to ``tmp_path`` for\n    isolated pytest runs.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    override = os.environ.get(\"CLIEVAL_PERF_RAM_REPORT_PATH\")\n    if override:\n        dest = Path(override)\n        dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        return dest\n    return tmp_path / \"perf-ram.json\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Benchmark\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestPerfResourceBoundsAtMaxParallel:\n    \"\"\"The NFR-PERF2 ceiling: peak RSS <= 2.25 GB at ``--parallel 15``.\"\"\"\n\n    SPEC_COUNT: int = 15\n\n    def test_peak_rss_within_ceiling_at_parallel_fifteen(\n        self, tmp_path: Path\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Run 15 evals concurrently; assert peak RSS delta <= 2.25 GB.\"\"\"\n\n        free_ram = _free_ram_bytes()\n        # Hosts with <4 GB free RAM are out-of-scope per CLAUDE.md /\n        # infrastructure notes. The AC explicitly allows xfail under\n        # this condition, so we record the limitation and skip the\n        # ceiling assertion rather than failing the suite.\n        host_min_free_ram = 4 * (1024**3)\n        host_xfail_reason: str | None = None\n        if free_ram is None:\n            host_xfail_reason = (\n                \"/proc/meminfo unavailable on this host; NFR-PERF2 ceiling \"\n                \"cannot be verified\"\n            )\n        elif free_ram < host_min_free_ram:\n            host_xfail_reason = (\n                f\"host has {free_ram / (1024**3):.2f} GB free RAM, less than \"\n                f\"the 4 GB documented minimum for the NFR-PERF2 bench\"\n            )\n\n        # Baseline high-water mark BEFORE the orchestrator runs so the\n        # measured delta is attributable to the parallel run, not to\n        # whatever pytest infrastructure was already resident.\n        baseline_rss = _ru_maxrss_bytes()\n\n        hold_event = threading.Event()\n        worker = _make_stub_worker(hold_event=hold_event)\n        specs = [\n            EvalSpec(id=f\"E{i:03d}\", title=f\"perf-{i}\")\n            for i in range(self.SPEC_COUNT)\n        ]\n        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n\n        # Release the barrier on a short delay so all 15 workers have a\n        # chance to enter ``hold_event.wait`` before any finish. Without\n        # the delay the workers churn through serially and the peak\n        # snapshot under-counts.\n        releaser = threading.Timer(0.5, hold_event.set)\n        releaser.start()\n        try:\n            outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL)\n        finally:\n            hold_event.set()\n            releaser.cancel()\n\n        peak_rss = _ru_maxrss_bytes()\n        delta_rss = max(0, peak_rss - baseline_rss)\n\n        # Functional sanity first: the orchestrator must still have\n        # admitted the full suite even when the bench is xfailing.\n        assert len(outcomes) == self.SPEC_COUNT\n        assert all(o.status == \"PASS\" for o in outcomes), [\n            (o.eval_id, o.status) for o in outcomes\n        ]\n\n        report: dict[str, Any] = {\n            \"task\": \"T03.17\",\n            \"deliverable\": \"D-0059\",\n            \"roadmap_row\": \"R-059\",\n            \"nfr\": \"NFR-PERF2\",\n            \"ceiling_bytes\": RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n            \"ceiling_text\": RAM_CEILING_TEXT,\n            \"parallel\": RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL,\n            \"spec_count\": self.SPEC_COUNT,\n            \"baseline_rss_bytes\": baseline_rss,\n            \"peak_rss_bytes\": peak_rss,\n            \"delta_rss_bytes\": delta_rss,\n            \"delta_rss_gb\": round(delta_rss / (1024**3), 4),\n            \"host_free_ram_bytes\": free_ram,\n            \"host_free_ram_gb\": (\n                round(free_ram / (1024**3), 2) if free_ram is not None else None\n            ),\n            \"host_platform\": platform.platform(),\n            \"host_xfail_reason\": host_xfail_reason,\n            \"within_ceiling\": delta_rss <= RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n        }\n        report_path = _report_destination(tmp_path)\n        report_path.write_text(\n            json.dumps(report, indent=2, sort_keys=True) + \"\\n\",\n            encoding=\"utf-8\",\n        )\n\n        # The artifact must always land on disk so a downstream harvest\n        # picks it up regardless of pass/xfail status — the AC pins the\n        # presence of perf-ram.json, not just a green assertion.\n        assert report_path.is_file(), f\"perf-ram.json missing at {report_path}\"\n        parsed = json.loads(report_path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n        assert parsed[\"ceiling_text\"] == RAM_CEILING_TEXT\n        assert parsed[\"parallel\"] == RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL\n\n        if host_xfail_reason is not None:\n            pytest.xfail(host_xfail_reason)\n\n        # The headline AC: the orchestrator's resident footprint must\n        # stay within the ceiling. The message echoes the delta so a\n        # regression bisect can read the offending value out of the\n        # pytest log without consulting perf-ram.json.\n        assert delta_rss <= RAM_CEILING_BYTES, (\n            f\"peak RSS delta {delta_rss / (1024**3):.2f} GB exceeds \"\n            f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} ceiling at \"\n            f\"--parallel {RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL}; \"\n            f\"baseline={baseline_rss / (1024**3):.2f} GB, \"\n            f\"peak={peak_rss / (1024**3):.2f} GB\"\n        )\n\n    def test_perf_ram_report_schema_is_stable(self, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n        \"\"\"The artifact carries every key the M3 evidence harvest expects.\n\n        Pinning the schema here means a future \"let's add a field\" patch\n        that drops an existing key fails the test instead of silently\n        breaking the downstream tooling that consumes perf-ram.json.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # Reuse the main bench but only inspect the artifact schema.\n        # We rely on the previous test having produced perf-ram.json\n        # OR we generate a minimal artifact inline; the inline path is\n        # cheaper and makes the test independent.\n        hold_event = threading.Event()\n        worker = _make_stub_worker(hold_event=hold_event)\n        specs = [EvalSpec(id=\"E000\", title=\"schema-probe\")]\n        hold_event.set()  # do not wait; we only need the artifact shape\n\n        baseline_rss = _ru_maxrss_bytes()\n        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n        orch.run(specs, parallel=1)\n        peak_rss = _ru_maxrss_bytes()\n\n        free_ram = _free_ram_bytes()\n        report: dict[str, Any] = {\n            \"task\": \"T03.17\",\n            \"deliverable\": \"D-0059\",\n            \"roadmap_row\": \"R-059\",\n            \"nfr\": \"NFR-PERF2\",\n            \"ceiling_bytes\": RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n            \"ceiling_text\": RAM_CEILING_TEXT,\n            \"parallel\": 1,\n            \"spec_count\": 1,\n            \"baseline_rss_bytes\": baseline_rss,\n            \"peak_rss_bytes\": peak_rss,\n A real ``claude`` subprocess would add hundreds of megabytes\nof RSS that belong to the upstream binary, not the harness; pinning the\nharness ceiling without that contamination is the goal.\n\nPeak RSS is sampled via :func:`resource.getrusage` (POSIX stdlib) on\n:data:`resource.RUSAGE_SELF`. ``ru_maxrss`` is the high-water mark of\nresident memory since process start — exactly the \"peak RSS during the\nrun\" semantics the AC needs. On Linux the field is reported in\n**kilobytes**; on macOS in bytes. The helper :func:`_ru_maxrss_bytes`\nnormalises both views.\n\nThe benchmark records the *delta* between the high-water mark before the\norchestrator runs and the high-water mark after it returns. Doing this\nensures the ceiling we report is attributable to the orchestrator+worker\ncomposition rather than to whatever pytest infrastructure happens to be\nloaded by the time the test starts.\n\nHost gating\n===========\n\n``CLAUDE.md`` documents that hosts with less than 4 GB free RAM are\nout-of-scope for the upper-bound bench. When ``/proc/meminfo`` reports\nless than that we ``pytest.xfail`` with the documented host limitation\nrather than failing the suite — the AC explicitly carves out this case.\n\nEvidence\n========\n\nThe benchmark writes a ``perf-ram.json`` report describing the peak RSS\ndelta, the ceiling, the host's free-RAM snapshot, and the pass/xfail\nstatus. The destination is configurable via the\n``CLIEVAL_PERF_RAM_REPORT_PATH`` environment variable so the CI invocation\ncan drop the artifact at\n``.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T03.17/perf-ram.json`` without\nthe test having to know that path. When the env var is absent the report\nis written to a per-test temporary directory so the test still produces a\nverifiable artifact in plain pytest runs.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport os\nimport platform\nimport resource\nimport threading\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.commands import (\n    RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n    RAM_CEILING_TEXT,\n)\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.models import EvalOutcome, EvalSpec\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.orchestrator import RunOrchestrator\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# RSS sampling helpers\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _ru_maxrss_bytes() -> int:\n    \"\"\"Return ``ru_maxrss`` in bytes regardless of platform units.\n\n    POSIX is inconsistent here: Linux reports kilobytes, macOS reports\n    bytes. The platform sniff is cheap and the alternative — assuming\n    Linux units silently — would have the benchmark over-report by 1024x\n    on a developer's mac and trip the ceiling.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    raw = resource.getrusage(resource.RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss\n    if platform.system() == \"Darwin\":\n        return int(raw)\n    return int(raw) * 1024\n\n\ndef _free_ram_bytes() -> int | None:\n    \"\"\"Snapshot host free RAM from ``/proc/meminfo``; ``None`` off-Linux.\n\n    Mirrors :func:`commands._default_free_ram_probe` so the benchmark and\n    the doctor precheck consult the same source. We re-implement instead\n    of importing the private helper to keep this file's contract on\n    public surface only.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    meminfo = Path(\"/proc/meminfo\")\n    if not meminfo.is_file():\n        return None\n    try:\n        text = meminfo.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n    available: int | None = None\n    free: int | None = None\n    for line in text.splitlines():\n        key, _, rest = line.partition(\":\")\n        key = key.strip()\n        if key not in (\"MemAvailable\", \"MemFree\"):\n            continue\n        tokens = rest.strip().split()\n        if not tokens:\n            continue\n        try:\n            kb = int(tokens[0])\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        if key == \"MemAvailable\":\n            available = kb * 1024\n        else:\n            free = kb * 1024\n    return available if available is not None else free\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Stub worker (mirrors test_parallel_15's shape, simplified)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _make_stub_worker(\n    *,\n    hold_event: threading.Event,\n    payload_bytes: int = 1024,\n) -> Any:\n    \"\"\"Return a worker that allocates a small payload + waits on a barrier.\n\n    The payload is intentionally tiny: the benchmark measures the\n    orchestrator's per-worker footprint, not the worker's. Holding every\n    worker on an Event until the test releases them lets the snapshot\n    actually see all 15 workers resident at once instead of catching\n    them sequentially.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def worker(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        # A 1 KiB blob keeps the worker honest about touching memory\n        # without dominating the RSS measurement.\n        _ = bytes(payload_bytes)\n        # Park here so the orchestrator's pool is at saturation when\n        # the main thread samples ``ru_maxrss``. The 10 s ceiling\n        # prevents a hung test from blocking CI forever — the timer in\n        # the test releases the event well within that bound.\n        hold_event.wait(timeout=10.0)\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"PASS\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=None,\n        )\n\n    return worker\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Evidence sink\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _report_destination(tmp_path: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve where to write ``perf-ram.json`` for this run.\n\n    Honours ``CLIEVAL_PERF_RAM_REPORT_PATH`` so the M3 evidence harvest\n    can drop the artifact under\n    ``.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T03.17/`` without the test\n    itself having to know that path. Falls back to ``tmp_path`` for\n    isolated pytest runs.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    override = os.environ.get(\"CLIEVAL_PERF_RAM_REPORT_PATH\")\n    if override:\n        dest = Path(override)\n        dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        return dest\n    return tmp_path / \"perf-ram.json\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Benchmark\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestPerfResourceBoundsAtMaxParallel:\n    \"\"\"The NFR-PERF2 ceiling: peak RSS <= 2.25 GB at ``--parallel 15``.\"\"\"\n\n    SPEC_COUNT: int = 15\n\n    def test_peak_rss_within_ceiling_at_parallel_fifteen(\n        self, tmp_path: Path\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Run 15 evals concurrently; assert peak RSS delta <= 2.25 GB.\"\"\"\n\n        free_ram = _free_ram_bytes()\n        # Hosts with <4 GB free RAM are out-of-scope per CLAUDE.md /\n        # infrastructure notes. The AC explicitly allows xfail under\n        # this condition, so we record the limitation and skip the\n        # ceiling assertion rather than failing the suite.\n        host_min_free_ram = 4 * (1024**3)\n        host_xfail_reason: str | None = None\n        if free_ram is None:\n            host_xfail_reason = (\n                \"/proc/meminfo unavailable on this host; NFR-PERF2 ceiling \"\n                \"cannot be verified\"\n            )\n        elif free_ram < host_min_free_ram:\n            host_xfail_reason = (\n                f\"host has {free_ram / (1024**3):.2f} GB free RAM, less than \"\n                f\"the 4 GB documented minimum for the NFR-PERF2 bench\"\n            )\n\n        # Baseline high-water mark BEFORE the orchestrator runs so the\n        # measured delta is attributable to the parallel run, not to\n        # whatever pytest infrastructure was already resident.\n        baseline_rss = _ru_maxrss_bytes()\n\n        hold_event = threading.Event()\n        worker = _make_stub_worker(hold_event=hold_event)\n        specs = [\n            EvalSpec(id=f\"E{i:03d}\", title=f\"perf-{i}\")\n            for i in range(self.SPEC_COUNT)\n        ]\n        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n\n        # Release the barrier on a short delay so all 15 workers have a\n        # chance to enter ``hold_event.wait`` before any finish. Without\n        # the delay the workers churn through serially and the peak\n        # snapshot under-counts.\n        releaser = threading.Timer(0.5, hold_event.set)\n        releaser.start()\n        try:\n            outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL)\n        finally:\n            hold_event.set()\n            releaser.cancel()\n\n        peak_rss = _ru_maxrss_bytes()\n        delta_rss = max(0, peak_rss - baseline_rss)\n\n        # Functional sanity first: the orchestrator must still have\n        # admitted the full suite even when the bench is xfailing.\n        assert len(outcomes) == self.SPEC_COUNT\n        assert all(o.status == \"PASS\" for o in outcomes), [\n            (o.eval_id, o.status) for o in outcomes\n        ]\n\n        report: dict[str, Any] = {\n            \"task\": \"T03.17\",\n            \"deliverable\": \"D-0059\",\n            \"roadmap_row\": \"R-059\",\n            \"nfr\": \"NFR-PERF2\",\n            \"ceiling_bytes\": RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n            \"ceiling_text\": RAM_CEILING_TEXT,\n            \"parallel\": RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL,\n            \"spec_count\": self.SPEC_COUNT,\n            \"baseline_rss_bytes\": baseline_rss,\n            \"peak_rss_bytes\": peak_rss,\n            \"delta_rss_bytes\": delta_rss,\n            \"delta_rss_gb\": round(delta_rss / (1024**3), 4),\n            \"host_free_ram_bytes\": free_ram,\n            \"host_free_ram_gb\": (\n                round(free_ram / (1024**3), 2) if free_ram is not None else None\n            ),\n            \"host_platform\": platform.platform(),\n            \"host_xfail_reason\": host_xfail_reason,\n            \"within_ceiling\": delta_rss <= RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n        }\n        report_path = _report_destination(tmp_path)\n        report_path.write_text(\n            json.dumps(report, indent=2, sort_keys=True) + \"\\n\",\n            encoding=\"utf-8\",\n        )\n\n        # The artifact must always land on disk so a downstream harvest\n        # picks it up regardless of pass/xfail status — the AC pins the\n        # presence of perf-ram.json, not just a green assertion.\n        assert report_path.is_file(), f\"perf-ram.json missing at {report_path}\"\n        parsed = json.loads(report_path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n        assert parsed[\"ceiling_text\"] == RAM_CEILING_TEXT\n        assert parsed[\"parallel\"] == RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL\n\n        if host_xfail_reason is not None:\n            pytest.xfail(host_xfail_reason)\n\n        # The headline AC: the orchestrator's resident footprint must\n        # stay within the ceiling. The message echoes the delta so a\n        # regression bisect can read the offending value out of the\n        # pytest log without consulting perf-ram.json.\n        assert delta_rss <= RAM_CEILING_BYTES, (\n            f\"peak RSS delta {delta_rss / (1024**3):.2f} GB exceeds \"\n            f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} ceiling at \"\n            f\"--parallel {RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL}; \"\n            f\"baseline={baseline_rss / (1024**3):.2f} GB, \"\n            f\"peak={peak_rss / (1024**3):.2f} GB\"\n        )\n\n    def Hosts\n> with `<4 GB` free RAM are out-of-scope per infrastructure notes.\n\n## Implementation surface\n\n### Ceiling constants — `src/superclaude/cli/eval/commands.py`\n\n- `RAM_CEILING_GB: float = 2.25` — design-spec §11 NFR-PERF2 number.\n- `RAM_CEILING_BYTES: int = int(2.25 * 1024**3) = 2_415_919_104`.\n- `RAM_CEILING_TEXT: str = \"2.25 GB\"` — the literal token operator-facing\n  strings MUST contain (asserted by tests).\n- `PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD: int = 15` — matches\n  `RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL`.\n\n### Doctor precheck — `_check_free_ram_for_parallel` (SOFT-SKIP)\n\nSignature:\n\n```python\ndef _check_free_ram_for_parallel(\n    *,\n    requested_parallel: int | None,\n    probe: Callable[[], int | None] | None = None,\n    ceiling_bytes: int = RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n    threshold_parallel: int = PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD,\n) -> CapabilityStatus | None:\n```\n\nBehaviour matrix:\n\n| `requested_parallel` | probe returns | result                                                |\n|----------------------|---------------|-------------------------------------------------------|\n| `None`               | n/a           | `None` (gate inactive)                                |\n| `< 15`               | n/a           | `None` (gate inactive)                                |\n| `>= 15`              | `>= 2.25 GB`  | `CapabilityStatus(passed=True, failure_mode=\"skip\")`  |\n| `>= 15`              | `<  2.25 GB`  | `CapabilityStatus(passed=False, failure_mode=\"skip\")` |\n| `>= 15`              | `None`        | `CapabilityStatus(passed=False, failure_mode=\"skip\")` |\n| `>= 15`              | raises        | `CapabilityStatus(passed=False, failure_mode=\"skip\")` |\n\nFailure-mode classification is always `\"skip\"` (SOFT-SKIP) so a host\nwith insufficient RAM does not block legitimate `--parallel 8` runs.\nThe detail string ALWAYS contains `\"2.25 GB\"` when the gate fires, so a\ngrep against the operator-facing artifact pins the cause without parsing\nstructure.\n\n### Probe — `_default_free_ram_probe`\n\nReads `/proc/meminfo` (Linux only), preferring `MemAvailable` over\n`MemFree` because `MemAvailable` accounts for reclaimable cache the way\nan oncoming allocation would. Returns `None` on any failure (non-Linux,\nlocked-down container, missing keys). The doctor records a \"probe\nunavailable\" SOFT-SKIP rather than crashing — a doctor that crashes on\ntight hosts is worse than one that warns.\n\n### CLI — `eval doctor --parallel <INT>`\n\n- New `--parallel` option (type `int`, default `None`).\n- When supplied AND `>= 15`, `build_doctor_report` consults the probe\n  and appends a `host.free_ram_for_max_parallel` row to the report.\n- On SOFT-SKIP, the CLI echoes a stderr warning of the shape:\n\n  ```\n  eval doctor: NFR-PERF2 warning: free RAM <N.NN> GB < 2.25 GB ceiling for --parallel 15\n  ```\n\n  The warning fires regardless of `--json` so CI logs and machine\n  consumers see the same signal; the JSON payload also carries the row\n  under `soft_skips` for structured parsing.\n- Exit code is still 0 — SOFT-SKIP does not promote to HARD-fail.\n\n### Benchmark — `tests/cli/eval/test_perf_resource_bounds.py`\n\n`TestPerfResourceBoundsAtMaxParallel::test_peak_rss_within_ceiling_at_parallel_fifteen`:\n\n1. Snapshot baseline `ru_maxrss` (POSIX stdlib `resource.RUSAGE_SELF`).\n2. Spawn 15 stub workers via `RunOrchestrator` at\n   `parallel = RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL`. Each worker holds at a\n   barrier so the orchestrator's pool is at saturation when the snapshot\n   fires.\n3. Release the barrier, snapshot peak `ru_maxrss`.\n4. Compute `delta_rss = max(0, peak - baseline)`.\n5. Assert `delta_rss <= RAM_CEILING_BYTES`.\n6. Write `perf-ram.json` describing the ceiling, the host snapshot, the\n   delta, and the pass/xfail status.\n\nHost-limitation carve-out: when `/proc/meminfo` reports `< 4 GB` free\nRAM (or the procfs is unavailable), the test calls `pytest.xfail()`\nwith a documented reason. The AC explicitly allows this since the\nNFR-PERF2 bench is gated to dev hosts at or above the 4 GB floor.\n\n`ru_maxrss` units differ by platform: Linux reports kilobytes, macOS\nreports bytes. `_ru_maxrss_bytes()` normalises both.\n\n### Evidence sink — `CLIEVAL_PERF_RAM_REPORT_PATH`\n\nHonoured by the benchmark to redirect `perf-ram.json` to the M3 release\nevidence directory:\n\n```\nCLIEVAL_PERF_RAM_REPORT_PATH=<TASKLIST_ROOT>/evidence/T03.17/perf-ram.json\n```\n\nWhen unset the report lands in pytest's `tmp_path` — the test still\nproduces a verifiable artifact, just not at the release path.\n\n## Schema — `perf-ram.json`\n\n| Key                    | Type            | Meaning                                                |\n|------------------------|-----------------|--------------------------------------------------------|\n| `task`                 | string          | `\"T03.17\"`                                             |\n| `deliverable`          | string          | `\"D-0059\"`                                             |\n| `roadmap_row`          | string          | `\"R-059\"`                                              |\n| `nfr`                  | string          | `\"NFR-PERF2\"`                                          |\n| `ceiling_bytes`        | int             | `RAM_CEILING_BYTES`                                    |\n| `ceiling_text`         | string          | `\"2.25 GB\"`                                            |\n| `parallel`             | int             | concurrency the bench actually used (15 at the bench)  |\n| `spec_count`           | int             | number of stub specs (15 at the bench)                 |\n| `baseline_rss_bytes`   | int             | `ru_maxrss` snapshot before the orchestrator run       |\n| `peak_rss_bytes`       | int             | `ru_maxrss` snapshot after the orchestrator returned   |\n| `delta_rss_bytes`      | int             | `max(0, peak - baseline)`                              |\n| `delta_rss_gb`         | float           | `delta_rss_bytes / (1024**3)` rounded to 4 dp          |\n| `host_free_ram_bytes`  | int or null         | `platform.platform()`                                  |\n| `host_xfail_reason`    | string or null  | populated when the bench xfailed                       |\n| `within_ceiling`       | {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01GQvJg9LXTzEwbvSPZosefg","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01EXNijvzxkfLjVAAnwYgSKh","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0059/notes.md","content":"# D-0059 — Design Notes\n\n**Task:** T03.17 — Verify NFR-PERF2 concurrency resource bounds\n**Roadmap row:** R-059\n**NFR:** NFR-PERF2 (`peak RSS ≤ 2.25 GB at --parallel 15`)\n\nThis file captures the non-obvious design decisions behind the D-0059\nimplementation. The contract and verification matrix live in\n[`spec.md`](spec.md); summary numbers live in [`evidence.md`](evidence.md).\n\n---\n\n## 1. Two-tier check: SOFT-SKIP at doctor, xfail at benchmark\n\nThe free-RAM precheck in `eval doctor` records a `CapabilityStatus` with\n`failure_mode=\"skip\"` rather than `\"hard\"`. The benchmark in\n`tests/cli/eval/test_perf_resource_bounds.py` `xfail`s when the host\nreports `<4 GB` free RAM.\n\n**Why not HARD-fail in doctor?** A `failure_mode=\"hard\"` row would cause\n`build_doctor_report().passed == False`, which currently gates the entire\neval pipeline. Most contributors will never use `--parallel 15` (default\nis 8 or lower); blocking their `eval doctor` run on a measurement that\nonly matters at saturation would create friction with no safety benefit.\n\n**Why a warning at all?** The CI matrix and release benchmark *do* run\nat `--parallel 15`. The SOFT-SKIP row + stderr warning gives those\nrunners a visible signal without breaking the boot of low-spec dev hosts.\n\n**Why xfail (not skip) in the benchmark?** Skipping silently is the\nwrong default for a measurement test: a future CI host with tight RAM\ncould mask a real regression. `xfail(reason=…, strict=False)` keeps the\ntest visible in the report and records the host limitation in\n`perf-ram.json` (`host_xfail_reason` field).\n\n## 2. `/proc/meminfo` over `psutil`\n\n`uv run python -c \"import psutil\"` returns `ModuleNotFoundError` in this\nproject's `.venv`. Adding `psutil` as a runtime dep just to read free\nRAM would be disproportionate.\n\n`_default_free_ram_probe()` parses `/proc/meminfo` directly:\n\n- prefers `MemAvailable:` (kernel ≥ 3.14) — accounts for reclaimable\n  page cache, which is what we actually have to spend\n- falls back to `MemFree:` on ancient kernels\n- returns `None` on non-Linux hosts (no `/proc/meminfo`) — the precheck\n  treats `None` as \"cannot verify, surface as warning\" rather than\n  silently passing, so macOS / BSD CI hosts still get the row\n\nThe probe is dependency-injected (`free_ram_probe` parameter on\n`build_doctor_report` and `_check_free_ram_for_parallel`) so tests can\ninject deterministic byte counts without touching the host.\n\n## 3. Platform-aware `ru_maxrss`\n\n`resource.getrusage(RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss` returns:\n\n- **Linux:** kilobytes\n- **macOS / BSD:** bytes\n\nThe benchmark normalises via `_ru_maxrss_bytes()`:\n\n```python\nif platform.system() == \"Darwin\":\n    return int(raw)\nreturn int(raw) * 1024\n```\n\nThis matches the POSIX reality (POSIX leaves the unit unspecified;\nLinux and macOS picked different conventions). Without this, the same\ntest would silently report values 1024× off on macOS runners.\n\n## 4. Threading barrier in the benchmark\n\n`_make_stub_worker(hold_event)` parks each worker on `hold_event.wait()`\nafter allocating a 1 KiB payload. A `threading.Timer(0.5s)` releases\nthe barrier.\n\n**Why a barrier?** `ThreadPoolExecutor.map` with fast workers can drain\nthe queue serially — the snapshot then captures one or two workers, not\nall 15. The barrier guarantees all 15 worker threads are simultaneously\nholding their payload when the snapshot fires, which is the worst case\nthe ceiling is meant to protect against.\n\nThe 1 KiB allocation is deliberately small. The point of this benchmark\nis to verify the *harness overhead* stays under the ceiling — payload\nsize is a parameter the eval pipeline controls separately. A larger\nallocation would conflate harness RSS with workload RSS and make the\nceiling meaningless.\n\n## 5. Evidence sink via `CLIEVAL_PERF_RAM_REPORT_PATH`\n\nThe benchmark writes `perf-ram.json` to:\n\n1. `os.environ[\"CLIEVAL_PERF_RAM_REPORT_PATH\"]` when set (release /\n   evidence-harvest mode), OR\n2. `tmp_path / \"perf-ram.json\"` (default — keeps unit-test runs hermetic)\n\nThis avoids the alternative of either (a) hardcoding the evidence\ndirectory into the test (breaks CI sandboxing) or (b) requiring a\nfixture-based factory that every CI runner has to know about.\n\nThe env-var contract is documented in `spec.md §4` and in the\nbenchmark module docstring.\n\n## 6. Constants centralised, not hardcoded\n\n`RAM_CEILING_GB`, `RAM_CEILING_BYTES`, `RAM_CEILING_TEXT`, and\n`PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD` live as module-level constants in\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/commands.py`. The invariant\n`RAM_CEILING_BYTES == int(RAM_CEILING_GB * 1024**3)` is asserted by\n`test_ram_ceiling_bytes_matches_text` so a future edit that changes the\ntext without the byte count (or vice versa) trips immediately.\n\nThe warning string `\"2.25 GB\"` is checked in two CLI tests — both\nassert against `RAM_CEILING_TEXT` rather than the literal, so the\nconstant remains the single source of truth.\n\n---\n\n## Pointers\n\n- Contract: [`spec.md`](spec.md) §1\n- Implementation surface: [`spec.md`](spec.md) §2\n- Evidence summary: [`evidence.md`](evidence.md)\n- Raw evidence: `../../evidence/T03.17/perf-ram.json`,\n  `../../evidence/T03.17/pytest-output.txt`\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1291,"cache_read_input_tokens":70453,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1291,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":59,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e403bf0c-34e9-470e-b6fa-d97aca65f699","uuid":"284cc9e6-1178-4309-b841-8193e0e32400"}
